## Current approved G2 JSON-shaped equality-filter characterization

Added a read-only raw-client probe sending the same JSON object as an equality value for scalar text `todos.title`. TrailBase client 0.14.3 emitted `filter[title][$eq]=[object Object]`; Supabase JS 2.117.2 emitted `title=eq.[object Object]`. Both requests succeeded with HTTP 200 and returned empty result sets. Complete `todos` rows and `todo_audit` snapshots stayed unchanged. This is installed-client coercion only; a future adapter must reject JSON-shaped scalar filter values before wire. No JSON-field/filter support or SDK contract expansion.

## Previous G7 acknowledgement and discarded-login cleanup follow-up

The maintainer completed interview `ef996dac-be47-4c9d-8ec6-a2dba294b5a1` and [approved both bounded investigations](evidence/phase-a-g7-acknowledgement-cleanup-directions.json). The test-only coordinator now attempts one local revocation of the exact genuine credential decoded from a stale login, preserving newer/sibling sessions and surfacing cleanup failure without replay. A fixed same-origin health acknowledgement candidate uses only Bearer authorization/omitted cookies; it remains separate from the coordinator's manual global path. Native/browser probes require real genuine/anonymous/invalid/expired revocation postconditions and reject foreign final URLs as native acknowledgement. An owned second-origin sink records only credential-presence booleans/counts.

At the initial follow-up source, [34 native proofs](evidence/phase-a-g7-ack-cleanup-proofs-node22.json) passed, including success, transport/genuine-405 cleanup failure and missing/truncated genuine HTTP delivery. All **12** new discarded-login cases passed across the three browsers, but [the complete browser run](evidence/phase-a-g7-ack-cleanup-initial-browser-node22.json) was **40 passed / 2 failed**. [Stage diagnostics](evidence/phase-a-g7-ack-cleanup-browser-diagnosis.json) preserved the same failures: Firefox/WebKit blocked the foreign redirect before any accepted GET reached the sink, whereas Chromium returned a readable response with no credentials. The original expectation that all engines must return a readable foreign response was a test assumption, not an auth/security requirement. Revised assertions keep every credential, independent refresh-revocation, cookie and cleanup check; they accept only the characterized TypeError/engine block and now count rejected preflights too. The [revised complete browser scope](evidence/phase-a-g7-ack-cleanup-revised-browser-node22.json) passed **42/42**. The all-method counter showed Chromium: one GET, no credentials; Firefox/WebKit: one rejected non-GET preflight each, no GET and no credentials. This fixture refuses preflight; permissive third-origin/custom-fetch/TLS/WAN coverage remains incomplete, so this is not a universal credential-stripping guarantee. All owned listeners closed.

Initial-source controls: [expiry 2/2](evidence/phase-a-g7-ack-cleanup-expiry-node22.json), [stock 82 passed/4 strict failures](evidence/phase-a-g7-ack-cleanup-all-node22.json), [candidate characterization 10/10](evidence/phase-a-g7-ack-cleanup-candidate-characterization-node22.json), [candidate SMTP 0/1](evidence/phase-a-g7-ack-cleanup-candidate-smtp-node22.json), [migration 1/1](evidence/phase-a-g7-ack-cleanup-migration-node22.json) and [fault cleanup 1/1](evidence/phase-a-g7-ack-cleanup-cleanup-node22.json). Every scope passed setup/cleanup. A genuine expired JWT also produced same-origin health 200 **without revoking either sibling refresh session**. A final HTTP 200 is not alone evidence of global revocation; the manual global coordinator remains unchanged and G7 stays blocked.

## Previous approved G5/G6/G7 directions and auth-scope evidence

After interview `1a566198-4ddd-4fb3-a40c-1e28035d3fa7` timed out, the maintainer explicitly instructed proceeding with its three answered choices. [Recorded approval](evidence/phase-a-auth-stream-policy-directions.json): guarded native-HTTP auth, minimal buffered authenticated Fetch/SSE, and common eventType/schema/table/new plus DELETE-key-only old payloads. These are policy directions only; no signoff, production SDK/Phase B, deployment, repin, identity repair, commit/push or public submission is authorized.

[All 25 test-only proofs](evidence/phase-a-auth-scopes-proofs-node22.json) and [27 Chromium/Firefox/WebKit cases](evidence/phase-a-auth-scopes-browser-node22.json) passed: explicit scopes/other-user isolation, invalid-scope/no-request safety, repeated/concurrent logout, both scopes' transport errors, late login at response/JSON completion, and old local logout preserving a new account. The proof's historical default remains **local**, not the future public global default.

Two observed G7 gaps need further policy input. Browser manual global GET produces `opaqueredirect`/status 0/no Location; the proof visibly errors although separate genuine refresh checks confirm both sibling sessions revoked. This is **not** a working browser-global API. A late successful login discarded after logout cannot restore local state, but its newly created remote refresh session remains usable until explicit owned-test cleanup. Neither result is normalized into success or repaired through global/sibling revocation.

Same-source controls: [full stock](evidence/phase-a-auth-scopes-all-node22.json) **73 passed / 4 strict failures**, [real expiry](evidence/phase-a-auth-scopes-expiry-node22.json) **2/2**, [candidate characterization](evidence/phase-a-auth-scopes-candidate-characterization-node22.json) **10/10**, [candidate SMTP](evidence/phase-a-auth-scopes-candidate-smtp-node22.json) **0/1**, [stock migration refusal](evidence/phase-a-auth-scopes-migration-node22.json) **1/1**, and [fault cleanup](evidence/phase-a-auth-scopes-cleanup-node22.json) **1/1**. All eight real scopes passed strict setup/owned cleanup and the local lock cleared; aggregate exit 1 preserves both G1 and both installed-client G7 failures plus candidate missing-mail recovery. No retries or private G1 tests entered stock `all`.

That source passed **40/40** units on pinned [Node 22](evidence/phase-a-auth-scopes-unit-node22.json) and [Node 24](evidence/phase-a-auth-scopes-unit-node24.json); static/docs/secret checks passed. The maintainer approved a narrow G2 mapped scalar/page policy: UUIDv4, declared booleans, safe-integer Numbers and declared text/null/default behavior; limit 0–1000 and nonnegative safe-integer offsets; reject unmapped/date/arbitrary BLOB/JSON/relation operations. This is policy approval, not SDK/G2 signoff. The maintainer also approved preserving G3 backend differences: native 403 for missing/foreign mutations versus reference 204/no-data, and backend constraint-error details; G3 remains unsigned. G4 direction is also approved: order mapped non-null fields, promise only tested ASCII text sorting, require caller tie-breakers, follow characterized lazy/repeated-builder and last-effective-bound semantics, and apply explicit bounds before exact `single`/`maybeSingle` cardinality without implicit `limit(1)`; G4 remains unsigned. For effective positive offset plus zero limit, the future adapter must resolve error/no data with an explicitly adapter-generated range error, not a fabricated native HTTP 416. One `gpt-6.1-sol` writer produced a three-file private draft. The initial attempt stopped before compilation because pinned vendor submodules were uninitialized; after exact gitlink restoration, Rust 1.95.0 compilation stopped on 11 preexisting unstable `std::assert_matches` uses. Both failures are preserved. Upstream-CI-pinned stable 1.98.1 was installed only in an owned private rustup home. Under that version, **23 native test executions / 22 distinct tests passed**; the parent independently reproduced them. A no-default-feature dev CLI was built from the exact source patch, and two owned six-image/loopback real-HTTP cases passed with cleanup: SMTP outage/recovery and concurrent case-insensitive signup preserving exactly one pending identity. Independent review found the protected-read check accepted arbitrary errors; it now requires an explicit 401/403 and the genuine rehearsal passed again. See [native tests](evidence/phase-a-private-g1-native-tests.json), [private build](evidence/phase-a-private-g1-build.json), [HTTP/SMTP evidence](evidence/phase-a-private-g1-http-smtp.json), [existing-depot upgrade evidence](evidence/phase-a-private-g1-upgrade.json), [ambiguous-depot refusal evidence](evidence/phase-a-private-g1-ambiguous-refusal.json) and [independent review](evidence/phase-a-private-g1-review.json). These are bounded private prototype results—not stock behavior, production/release readiness, deployment approval or G1 signoff. One existing-depot upgrade and one real ambiguous-depot refusal/rollback passed; the confirmed identity survived one additional private-binary restart, and the SQLite optimizer-stat metadata delta is recorded separately. One local stock CLI SQLite backup/restore cycle passed; off-host disaster recovery, further restart cycles, multi-server concurrency, lifecycle/security and cross-platform CI remain open. The isolated checkout is uncommitted on the pinned baseline. Existing four-hour resend throttling is unchanged; a failed public resend is not immediately retryable. No G1/G4 gate signoff is inferred.

**Accepted realtime contracts:** adopt TrailBase's intended connection-scoped SSE authentication. Credentials are checked at establishment; access lasts for that connection's lifetime, including after JWT expiry. A new connection with an expired token is denied. Upstream [#299](https://github.com/trailbaseio/trailbase/issues/299) was [answered and closed](https://github.com/trailbaseio/trailbase/issues/299#issuecomment-5969636164) with explicit user approval. This supersedes the earlier server-expiry/revalidation requirement, not native owner rules. Historical failed expiry reports below retain their original outcomes; they do not establish a current defect. The maintainer subsequently approved documenting native owner-only DELETE versus Supabase key-only foreign DELETE visibility in interview `186fd6d9-0e51-4c33-a864-8e099ba8cd26`. Identifier confidentiality is not portable; protected row values remain private and native rules are unchanged. These two contract differences no longer block G6; complete payload/CDC/lifecycle/resource evidence and full signoff remain pending.

[Revised-contract Node 22 evidence](evidence/phase-a-connection-lifetime-node22.json): **2 expiry cases passed**, with strict setup and owned cleanup passed. The original genuine JWT became unusable for ordinary reads and a new stream; the already-established stream still delivered the expected freshly written row. The optional local deadline abort proof also passed. **37 unit checks** and `npm run check` passed on Node 22.23.2. This is contract characterization, not a repaired server-expiry guarantee or SDK/gate signoff.

Linux [run 37127182103](https://github.com/burggraf/trailbase-supabase/actions/runs/37127182103) at `eac36ce` passed both supported-Node unit jobs, [2 revised-contract expiry cases](evidence/phase-a-ci-linux-connection-lifetime-expiry.json), [12 browser cases](evidence/phase-a-ci-linux-connection-lifetime-browser.json) and [fault cleanup](evidence/phase-a-ci-linux-connection-lifetime-lifecycle.json). [Stock remains 53 passed/5 failed](evidence/phase-a-ci-linux-connection-lifetime-all.json): duplicate-pending confirmation, SMTP recovery, reference foreign DELETE key, and two native late-response/logout races. Every scope reached strict setup verification and passed cleanup. The job is correctly red; this run predates the migration proof additions.

## G2/G4 scalar-domain follow-up

`npm run test:phase-a -- domains` runs the isolated stock scalar probes. [Four local real cases](evidence/phase-a-scalar-domains-node22.json) passed: all six numeric comparisons with exact golden IDs; reserved/Unicode/combining text as literal values; null inequality plus declared boolean/UUID filters; and numeric ties with explicit keys plus lowercase ASCII words ascending/descending. The latest 26-case unit report also passed; cleanup passed.

This is a bounded installed-client characterization, not an SDK contract or broad text-collation guarantee. The later boundary follow-up below covers unsafe decoding, nullable defaults and builder isolation. Adapter option validation, broad collation guarantees and remote CI remain incomplete. G1 blockers and all maintainer signoff requirements are unchanged.

## G2/G4 boundary follow-up

`npm run test:phase-a -- boundaries` runs the isolated stock boundary probes. [Five real cases](evidence/phase-a-query-boundaries-node22.json) and the latest 26-case unit report passed locally on Node 22; cleanup passed. Exact observations:

- `9007199254740991` round-trips as a safe number. Native installed-client decoding preserves `9007199254740993` as bigint; the reference installed client returns `9007199254740992` even though real PostgREST wire data contains the exact original integer. This does not widen the planned portable safe-integer domain.
- On nullable text, equality with JS `null` selects the literal string `"null"`, not SQL NULL. The future adapter must reject null comparison as planned; `.is()` remains deferred.
- Numeric NaN/infinities/fractions, an unknown column and a malformed UUID fail in both pinned backends; fixture row counts remain unchanged. This is backend rejection evidence, not proof of a future adapter rejecting before requests.
- Native nullable ascending order places NULL first; reference default ascending places it last. Explicit reference `nullsFirst:true` aligns this fixture, but nullable ordering remains outside the initial portable contract. Native mixed-case/Unicode order matches binary UTF-8; the reference fixture produces reversible ascending/descending results. No broad cross-database collation claim is made. Composed/decomposed accents remain distinct literal predicates.
- Reference query construction is lazy; repeated awaits issue separate real requests. Independently constructed builders isolate, but branching the same builder mutates it. Native `listOp` is lazy, repeated `.query()` calls issue requests, independent options isolate, and mutation of shared options affects a previously constructed operation.

G2's bounded scalar/page direction and G4's ordering/execution/cardinality directions are maintainer-approved; G2/G4 remain in progress. The latest [stock pagination suite](evidence/phase-a-g2-safe-range-overflow-node22.json) passed 7/7 with cleanup, covering malformed bounds, last-set range semantics, zero-width errors, out-of-range pages/counts, unsafe offsets, and a safe offset whose inclusive page end overflows integer safety. Both installed clients still send that request; the approved adapter contract rejects it before wire. The [domain/order suite](evidence/phase-a-g2-g4-domains-node22.json) passed 4/4 with cleanup: numeric predicates, literal Unicode filters, null inequality, declared boolean/UUID filters, and ASCII ordering. The [boundary suite](evidence/phase-a-g2-g4-boundaries-node22.json) passed 5/5 with cleanup, covering unsafe integer decoding, literal `null` text filters, malformed input errors, null-order divergence, and lazy/repeated builder execution. The latest [characterization run](evidence/phase-a-g4-cardinality-node22.json) remained red at 9/10 on strict G1, but its G2/G4 cardinality and repeated-builder case passed, covering zero/one/many `.single()`/`.maybeSingle()` and repeat execution. Both clients send `offset=9007199254740992&limit=1` and return an empty page; this is observation, not safe-integer validation. Stock inclusive-end overflow and initial repeated-order/call-order cases are characterized; adapter rejection/request-count tests, unsupported date/BLOB/JSON/relation shapes and nullable/reference-option rejection remain incomplete. No SDK implementation/signoff or G1 resolution is implied.

## G3/G4 update and repeated-order follow-up

[Focused stock evidence](evidence/phase-a-update-order-followup-node22.json) passed database **8/8** and boundaries **6/6**, with cleanup. Rejected unique/not-null/CHECK/type updates preserved complete target/control rows, including a valid note change submitted alongside the invalid field. Native SQL-constraint updates return 500, unlike native insert 400; reference error details remain backend-specific. Native updates ignore unknown fields and apply known fields; reference rejects the entire request with 400/PGRST204. The approved adapter must reject unmapped fields before wire. Repeated sort keys retain first-key precedence; order/range call order selected the same page. [Initial 400-status assumptions failed](evidence/phase-a-update-order-initial-assumptions-node22.json) and remain recorded.

The stock `all` runner now excludes the private G1 rehearsal files; explicit private scopes still select them. A unit regression and actual Vitest file selection check cover this harness isolation fix. No private auth tests or source patch are being included in stock evidence.

## Historical G5/G6 streaming follow-up — stricter DELETE-key contract (superseded)

`npm run test:phase-a -- streaming` now includes the expanded parser and two-owner cases. [Stock Node 22 evidence](evidence/phase-a-stream-isolation-node22.json): **3 passed, 1 failed**; latest 26 unit cases passed and owned fixture cleanup passed. It is not a green streaming/security suite.

- Native real UTF-8 INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE and two-owner isolation passed. Foreign INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE followed by own-event barriers did not appear in the owner reader; foreign direct read was denied. Both owners' final tables were empty. Reader cancellation completed; this does not prove every server-side stream resource/expiry condition.
- Whole-frame replay preserved a captured Unicode payload; byte-fragmented replay still failed/lost events. Synthetic sequence metadata over captured real payloads produced one loss callback for a gap inside one transport chunk but **zero** for the same gap split into whole-frame chunks. The installed parser resets sequence tracking per transform invocation. Explicit synthetic loss status produced a callback. These are deterministic installed-parser observations, not actual network fragmentation/loss/expiry proof or a working fallback.
- Reference basic own-row events and channel cleanup passed. In the strict two-owner test, INSERT/UPDATE own-event barriers and direct RLS invisibility passed, but the next DELETE event carried the **foreign owner's primary key**. Both owners' tables were empty and channel cleanup completed. The strict failure remains, including no leaked-row acceptance or event-discard workaround.

At this historical checkpoint, G6 was blocked on an explicit payload/security compatibility decision, now resolved by the approved backend difference above. This is the pinned reference backend's DELETE limitation, **not a demonstrated native TrailBase owner-isolation failure**; never weaken native access rules to mimic it. No broad security/CVE claim is made. G5 still requires a reviewed transport fallback and real network/browser corpus. G6/G7 still require expiration, revocation, renewal, anonymous, browser/storage/timer/race and resource evidence; the later G7 subset below adds local owned-storage and response-race observations. Maintainer signoff remains absent.

## G7 lifecycle follow-up — late-response/logout blockers

`npm run test:phase-a -- auth-lifecycle` adds real-response-gated lifecycle probes. [Stock Node 22 evidence](evidence/phase-a-auth-lifecycle-node22.json): **8 passed, 2 strict failures**. The shared async deadline has a runnable unit check; latest unit total is **27 passed**. [Re-run stream controls](evidence/phase-a-stream-isolation-node22.json) remain 3 passed/1 reference DELETE failure. Cleanup passed for both scopes.

- Native hydration initially exposes cached JWT claims; real revoked-session status validation subsequently clears cached user/tokens. Protected anonymous access is denied with HTTP 403. The [initial run](evidence/phase-a-auth-lifecycle-initial-status-mismatch.json) had two extra harness failures assuming HTTP 401 for that protected endpoint; status assertions were corrected to the observed HTTP 403 without weakening local-state or access-denial requirements.
- Two simultaneous native forced refreshes generate **two** real requests. Reference concurrent refresh is single-flight in this tested fixture, preserving a usable real session.
- **Native late refresh and status-validation responses restore cached user/tokens after logout.** Each test holds a complete genuine successful response, logs out while it is in flight, proves the refresh session was revoked on the actual server, then releases the response. The no-local-session assertion fails. No token/session/response was fabricated. These are installed-client state races, not demonstrated server-side refresh-revocation failures or privilege escalation.
- Reference late refresh after logout is discarded with `AuthRefreshDiscardedError`; no later `TOKEN_REFRESHED` event appears and protected access remains denied.
- Injected client-transport logout faults clear local state on both clients while the undelivered remote revocation leaves refresh usable. Native logout still returns `true`; reference logout exposes an error. These controlled transport faults are not proof of a real backend/network outage.
- Reference owned persistent storage hydrates a genuine session, validates it with real `getUser`, and reflects shared-store logout. An injected storage-write failure is observable, installs no local session, leaves protected access denied, and recovers with real login. No browser/cross-tab/automatic-timer parity or native storage implementation is claimed.

G7 is blocked on the two strict native state-race regressions and a reviewed upstream/future-wrapper mitigation path. No production guard or SDK implementation was added. G1/G6 blockers remain intact.

[Scope/limits](PROOF_INVESTIGATIONS.md) are part of the evidence source hash. No SDK implementation, deployment or gate signoff was approved.

- [35 unit checks](evidence/phase-a-unit-node22.json), including eight parser/property/malformed/cancellation cases: passed.
- [Eight genuine native proof cases](evidence/phase-a-coordination-proofs-node22.json): passed. The decoder preserved real UTF-8 INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE under deterministic post-HTTP byte rechunking and aborted a pending read. The auth proof single-flighted refresh, discarded late refresh/status responses held at both response and JSON completion, preserved a newer account's flight when an old one settled, and exposed undelivered logout while clearing locally.
- [Six three-browser infrastructure controls](evidence/phase-a-proof-browser-controls-node22.json) and [injected failure cleanup](evidence/phase-a-proof-cleanup-node22.json): passed. Those earlier browser controls did **not** execute the proof modules; the later browser follow-up below does.
- [Full stock suite](evidence/phase-a-proof-full-stock-node22.json): **43 passed, 5 failed**, all required G1/G6/G7 regressions still strict; browser stage not reached in the full run. Cleanup passed for every scope.

It was corrected to the original proof session's captured genuine headers; assertions were not relaxed. An account-switch case was then added, giving eight final real cases.

The proof is test-only and does not patch `initClient` or create Supabase sessions. Unsafe event integers are rejected under an explicit buffer/framing limit; automatic storage/timers/reconnection and full network/browser/security/schema handling remain incomplete. G1/G6/G7 remain blocked on the raw baseline and required decisions; G5 remains in progress. The following explicit expiry follow-up adds a native baseline blocker. Network/browser/resource checks still require completion.

## Actual native expiry — protected stream delivery persists

`npm run test:phase-a -- expiry` uses a fresh owned **short-native-auth** profile with a 3-second native JWT TTL. The [strict expiry report](evidence/phase-a-native-expiry-node22.json) failed: initial raw protected HTTP worked, genuine wall-clock expiry (including actual server grace) later denied the original JWT, but its previously opened stream delivered a newly written protected row from a freshly refreshed writer. The then-required close/denial contract failed; that requirement is now superseded by the connection-scoped contract above. This is not simulated expiry, JWT editing, reference expiry parity or a severity/CVE claim.

Default-profile controls under the same source passed: [8 proof cases](evidence/phase-a-expiry-default-proof-controls-node22.json), [6 browser infrastructure cases](evidence/phase-a-expiry-default-browser-controls-node22.json), [injected-failure cleanup](evidence/phase-a-expiry-default-cleanup-node22.json), and latest 36 unit checks. All owned cleanup passed. Reports/context/owner records label the native auth profile; default and candidate lifetimes are unchanged. The expiry corpus is outside the default `all` directory and has its own required CI step, as described in [proof investigation scope](PROOF_INVESTIGATIONS.md).

At that historical checkpoint G6 was blocked on both established-stream expiry semantics and reference foreign DELETE-key exposure; both contract differences are now explicitly accepted, without a complete G6 signoff. Owner-only native behavior is the chosen investigation direction, not a claim that native streams already enforce expiry. G5/G7 test-only proofs do not repair upstream behavior. No new upstream issue was submitted; both existing installed-client reports remain drafts. The following browser follow-up executes the proof modules and adds setup diagnostics. Network/resource checks remain incomplete.

## Genuine browser reference-SDK persistence/cross-tab follow-up

[Current-source browser evidence](evidence/phase-a-browser-storage-node22.json) passed **12 cases**: the existing six infrastructure/three native proof cases plus one genuine installed reference SDK persistence/cross-tab case per Chromium/Firefox/WebKit. Real password login persists the session under an owned unique key; a new document hydrates it and validates `getUser()`/owner rows remotely. A second tab hydrates the same store, observes `SIGNED_OUT` after real first-tab logout, sees no local session/storage and receives strict anonymous SELECT permission denial. Its retained genuine refresh token is rejected by the real service without restoring state. Only booleans leave browser evaluation; auto-refresh is deliberately disabled, so no background timer/retry or adapter storage-parity claim is made.

The installed pinned UMD is copied into the owned disposable public fixture only; no dependency/bundle/public adapter was added. [37 unit cases](evidence/phase-a-unit-node22.json), [two HTTP controls](evidence/phase-a-storage-network-controls-node22.json) and [fault cleanup](evidence/phase-a-storage-cleanup-node22.json) passed. ## Filtered exact counts and out-of-range pages

[Two genuine count probes](evidence/phase-a-count-pagination-node22.json) passed: bounded page totals and reference HEAD/native zero-body probes retain four filtered own-row matches and exclude foreign owners; genuine zero-match queries count zero. Existing cap/inclusive-range/call-order tests were reused, not duplicated.

An important boundary differs: native offset past the matching rows succeeds with empty records and `total_count: 0` even though four matches remain, while reference exact-count range returns HTTP **416**, error/no data and SDK `count: null`; its genuine HEAD wire still exposes `Content-Range: */4`. This is characterization, not approval to treat the native zero as a portable total or to hide the reference error. Count/head options are explicitly unsupported in the initial L1-05 subset, so no count-emulation strategy is needed. Ordinary no-count empty pages are being characterized separately; G2 still requires complete relevant matrices and named signoff.

Current source [37 unit cases](evidence/phase-a-unit-node22.json), [12 browser controls](evidence/phase-a-pagination-browser-controls-node22.json) and [fault cleanup](evidence/phase-a-pagination-cleanup-node22.json) passed. [Full stock](evidence/phase-a-pagination-full-stock-node22.json) is **47 passed/5 failed**, with both count probes included and the same raw regressions. Every scope cleaned up. Latest native-expiry failure remains separately required, not repaired by count/browser successes.

## Genuine auth proof failure and JSON transport follow-up

The existing coordinator was reused **unchanged**. [11 native proof cases](evidence/phase-a-auth-failure-proofs-node22.json) passed, including three new bounded cases: a genuinely revoked refresh credential produces one shared HTTP 401, clears cache, and requires real relogin for recovery; a held old account's genuine 401 cannot clear a newer account or its flight; and actual connection destruction after 32 original successful refresh-JSON bytes fails both waiters, preserves only prior genuine cache, releases the reader/listener/flight, and permits a genuine retry. No response status, session or JSON body was fabricated. This does not prove rotating-provider parity, general retries, expiry timers or complete server accounting.

This bounded subset does not certify arbitrary slow consumers, WAN behavior or native server resource accounting.

## Historical client-only genuine JWT deadline proof

[Explicit expiry evidence](evidence/phase-a-native-client-deadline-node22.json) is **1 passed/1 failed**. Under the historical server-expiry contract, the original assertion failed: an already established native stream delivers a protected row after the original genuine JWT is denied for a new ordinary HTTP read. The separate positive case uses standard `AbortSignal.timeout` against a genuinely minted three-second JWT's `exp`; it aborts visibly/releases the reader at nominal expiry, subsequently observes genuine HTTP denial including server grace, confirms fresh-writer row integrity, and emits nothing from the closed reader. Same-host clock only; no fake clock/token editing or fabricated response. This is not server authorization enforcement, clock-skew negotiation, background-browser scheduling, renewal/reconnect or a release-approved lifecycle policy.

Current-source [37 unit cases](evidence/phase-a-unit-node22.json), [14 default proofs](evidence/phase-a-deadline-default-proof-controls-node22.json), [12 browsers](evidence/phase-a-deadline-default-browser-controls-node22.json) and [fault cleanup](evidence/phase-a-deadline-default-cleanup-node22.json) passed. [Default stock](evidence/phase-a-deadline-full-stock-node22.json) remains **53 passed/5 failed**. Every scope cleaned up. The raw expiry failure was not skipped, weakened or counted as a repaired server guarantee.

Current-source Linux [run 37101664560](https://github.com/burggraf/trailbase-supabase/actions/runs/37101664560) at `8b089da` passed both supported-Node unit jobs and [12 browsers](evidence/phase-a-ci-linux-client-deadline-browser.json), reproducing [stock 53/5](evidence/phase-a-ci-linux-client-deadline-all.json) and [explicit expiry 1/1](evidence/phase-a-ci-linux-client-deadline-expiry.json)—the standard client deadline passed, while raw server-expiry delivery remained strictly failed. [Fault cleanup](evidence/phase-a-ci-linux-client-deadline-lifecycle.json), strict six-service image/loopback verification and all owned cleanup passed. The required job correctly stays red. These are bounded proof/harness outcomes, not unimplemented SDK/security/release coverage.

Upstream subsequently clarified connection-scoped lifetime semantics in #299; the project's contract now adopts them. Remaining schema/timer/backpressure/server-resource matrices still apply. No public SDK implementation, deployment recommendation or gate approval. The maintainer explicitly approved submitting **only** the neutral stream-lifetime question in the answered response from interview `f900e981-5a7b-4fac-9763-2214d5bcbdd1`; both installed-client reports remain drafts.

The [stream lifetime question](UPSTREAM_STREAM_LIFETIME_ISSUE.md) was submitted as [trailbaseio/trailbase#299](https://github.com/trailbaseio/trailbase/issues/299). The published body was verified against the prepared sanitized text and uses absolute reproduction/evidence links pinned to `8b089da25057b6a74f066402634230423caf0c85`. It asks about intended connection-scoped authentication/expiry/re-auth semantics, makes no severity or foreign-row claim, and distinguishes client timers from server authorization enforcement. This is upstream clarification, not a compatibility exception, permission change, server fork or Phase B authorization. #298 remains the separate signup-recovery report; the raw regressions stay strict and red.

## G7 terminal-revocation ordering — test-only proof fix

Four S04/S08 regressions hold a genuine successful refresh/status response at response or JSON completion, then remotely revoke the real session and observe the other endpoint's terminal 401. The held success must not restore state after that denial. The original proof cleared tokens without advancing its generation on terminal denial, unlike logout; this was a proof-coordinator gap, not a new installed-client or server claim.

[Initial report](evidence/phase-a-terminal-revocation-initial-fixture-failures-node22.json): 14 passed/4 failed, cleanup passed. Two JSON-stage cases genuinely resolved rather than rejecting as stale; two response-stage cases failed account setup because their fixture email labels exceeded the email local-part limit. Only those labels were shortened. The [complete pre-fix run](evidence/phase-a-terminal-revocation-pre-fix-node22.json) then failed all four unchanged race assertions specifically because genuine late success resolved instead of rejecting as stale; the other 14 proofs passed and cleanup passed. Cache clearing now uses one generation-invalidating function shared by login, logout and validation/revocation transitions. Four equivalent cases were added for each browser. [All 18 post-fix proofs](evidence/phase-a-terminal-revocation-proofs-node22.json) and [24 browser cases](evidence/phase-a-terminal-revocation-browser-node22.json) passed, including all four genuine terminal-denial races in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit. [Two expiry cases](evidence/phase-a-terminal-revocation-expiry-node22.json), [two real HTTP controls](evidence/phase-a-terminal-revocation-network-node22.json), [fault cleanup](evidence/phase-a-terminal-revocation-lifecycle-node22.json), 37 unit checks and `npm run check` passed. Every scope passed setup and cleanup.

[Full stock is 58 passed/4 strict failures](evidence/phase-a-terminal-revocation-all-node22.json): the same two G1 and two installed-client G7 regressions. The change fixes only the test-only coordinator; no installed-client repair, production implementation, transient-error policy or gate signoff is inferred. Linux [run 37131271545](https://github.com/burggraf/trailbase-supabase/actions/runs/37131271545) at `5ba3e25` reproduced [stock 58/4](evidence/phase-a-ci-linux-terminal-revocation-all.json) and passed [all 24 browser cases](evidence/phase-a-ci-linux-terminal-revocation-browser.json), including every new proof race. Both 37-case supported-Node unit jobs, [two expiry cases](evidence/phase-a-ci-linux-terminal-revocation-expiry.json), [native migration preflight](evidence/phase-a-ci-linux-terminal-revocation-auth-migration.json), [10 candidate characterization cases](evidence/phase-a-ci-linux-terminal-revocation-candidate-characterization.json) and [fault cleanup](evidence/phase-a-ci-linux-terminal-revocation-lifecycle.json) passed. [Candidate SMTP](evidence/phase-a-ci-linux-terminal-revocation-candidate-smtp.json) remains strictly failed. All seven real scopes passed setup and cleanup; the job correctly remains red. This evidence predates the query-boundary expansion.

## G2/G4 malformed and mixed page bounds — limited adapter direction approved

The [initial expanded run](evidence/phase-a-page-bounds-initial-assumption-failures-node22.json) has 2 passed/3 failed, cleanup passed: assumptions that reference malformed bounds always error and mixed zero-width pages always succeed were incorrect. [First diagnostic run](evidence/phase-a-page-bounds-initial-diagnostic-node22.json) and [private metadata-capture run](evidence/phase-a-page-bounds-private-diagnostic-node22.json) retain those failures; neither is a green characterization or SDK result. Temporary diagnostic code was removed after capturing only status/code/row-count metadata privately.

Raw observations: native NaN pagination is omitted; reference `limit(NaN)` succeeds with its default page while `range(NaN,NaN)` succeeds empty. Reference fractional/infinite limits can also succeed with a default page; negative limits error. All these values remain outside the adapter's planned input domain. Ordinary no-count pages beyond the end succeed empty on both backends. Reference positive-offset zero-width pages return 416/PGRST103, whereas direct native zero-limit reads succeed empty. [All five revised pagination cases](evidence/phase-a-page-bounds-pagination-node22.json) passed with exact golden rows, request bounds, errors and database postconditions. [Five boundary cases](evidence/phase-a-page-bounds-boundaries-node22.json), [four scalar-domain cases](evidence/phase-a-page-bounds-domains-node22.json), [24 browser cases](evidence/phase-a-page-bounds-browser-node22.json), [fault cleanup](evidence/phase-a-page-bounds-lifecycle-node22.json), 37 unit checks and `npm run check` passed on Node 22.23.2. Every scope passed strict setup and cleanup. [Full stock is 61 passed/4 failed](evidence/phase-a-page-bounds-all-node22.json) with the same two G1 and two G7 regressions; no strict auth assertion changed. This verifies raw characterization, not future input validation or implemented adapter behavior. Linux [run 37133365644](https://github.com/burggraf/trailbase-supabase/actions/runs/37133365644) at `f26efb7` reproduced [stock 61/4](evidence/phase-a-ci-linux-page-bounds-all.json), with [24 browser cases](evidence/phase-a-ci-linux-page-bounds-browser.json), [2 expiry cases](evidence/phase-a-ci-linux-page-bounds-expiry.json), [native migration preflight](evidence/phase-a-ci-linux-page-bounds-auth-migration.json), [10 candidate characterization cases](evidence/phase-a-ci-linux-page-bounds-candidate-characterization.json) and [fault cleanup](evidence/phase-a-ci-linux-page-bounds-lifecycle.json) passed. Both supported-Node unit jobs passed 37 cases. [Candidate SMTP](evidence/phase-a-ci-linux-page-bounds-candidate-smtp.json) still failed. All seven real scopes passed strict setup and cleanup; the job is correctly red. This evidence predates the documentation of the newly approved direction.

The follow-up instruction after interview `6f74221d-1b37-456b-af5d-261ea4372430` timed out authorizes only the bounded private G1 prototype and the recommended G4 adapter-origin range-error policy. No production implementation, deployed server fork, baseline change, public upstream follow-up or gate signoff is authorized. Count/head remain unsupported; no speculative count strategy is being built.

[progress.json](progress.json) is the authoritative status/signoff ledger. Run `npm run progress` to see Phase A deliverables, missing checks, open gates, and ordered next steps. Read this file first after a restart, then the [implementation plan](LEVEL1_PLAN.md) and [test matrix](TEST_PLAN.md).

## Status and completion rules

| Status | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| planned | Contract/test requirements exist; implementation has not started |
| in-progress | Work or characterization started; see `missing` and evidence |
| implemented | Code exists; required verification is still incomplete |
| verified | Required checks for the recorded scope/environment passed; not maintainer approval |
| signed-off | Required tests/decisions complete; named maintainer reviewed current evidence |
| blocked | A concrete failing prerequisite/decision prevents progress |

**Completed means signed-off, not merely implemented or locally passing.** A harness result does not verify an SDK feature. The license is MIT by the maintainer's explicit instruction; this is not approval of the seven compatibility exceptions. The npm project remains private, with a provisional unscoped name; no package has been published.

### Stable tracking IDs

- `A01`: restartable ledger, validation, evidence and handoff process.
- `A02`: exact tools/dependencies/service images/runtime targets, MIT license and package naming.
- `A03`: disposable TrailBase/Supabase/SMTP, checked-in schema/config and lifecycle.
- `A04`: fixture/database/permission and cleanup-failure checks.
- `A05`: upstream characterization for G1–G7 and three-browser infrastructure checks.
- `A06`: CI execution and required gates, with unimplemented SDK layers explicitly incomplete.
- `L1-01`–`L1-27`: [feature matrix](TEST_PLAN.md); statuses tracked individually in JSON.
- `G1`–`G7`: [research decisions](RESEARCH.md); observations are not approval.

| Evidence | Result |
| --- | --- |
| [Unit guards](evidence/phase-a-unit-node22.json) | 22 cases, including 1000 seeded UUID vectors |
| [Real upstream/browser infrastructure](evidence/phase-a-all-node22.json) | 16 database/auth/query/stream cases and 6 browser scenarios (Chromium/Firefox/WebKit); includes 10 pgTAP assertions |
| [Injected setup failure](evidence/phase-a-lifecycle-node22.json) | Cleanup passed after both servers had started |
| `npm run check` | TypeScript, JavaScript syntax, docs/traceability, evidence freshness and secret scan passed |

Confirmed duplicate-signup probes now verify no new session, preserved original passwords, matching wrong/unknown-login error contracts, and disabled phone signup. The reference fixture explicitly disables both email and SMS auto-confirmation; phone signup stays disabled. The initial failed probe is retained in [historical failure evidence](evidence/phase-a-g1-initial-failure.json), not counted green.

No owned fixture containers/volumes/networks remained. **[Earlier-foundation Linux x64 CI passed](https://github.com/burggraf/trailbase-supabase/actions/runs/37083854601)**: static/unit checks on Node 22.23.2 and 24.21.0, plus real backends/all three browsers/injected-failure cleanup on Node 22. Sanitized earlier-foundation CI reports are preserved in `docs/evidence/phase-a-ci-*.json`. The earlier foundation CI is historical and remains traceable through the ledger/Git history. Node 24 real-backend/browser execution is not claimed. Remaining characterization, full SDK layers and maintainer signoff are still pending. These are **foundation results, not completion of Phase A or SDK compatibility**.

## What exists now

- Private, MIT-licensed npm harness with exact dependency lockfile and TrailBase release archive checksums.
- Exact Supabase CLI and first-boot service image/digest lock; image drift fails setup rather than adopting `latest`.
- Owner-protected UUID/integer/nonstandard-key fixtures, defaults/constraints, read-only views, confirmation-required auth and local Mailpit SMTP.
- Real upstream tests for signup/confirmation, UUID/boolean/default representations, unknown filters, 50/1000/1001 pagination, range/cardinality/builder behavior, forbidden/missing writes and two-session logout.
- Real native/Supabase change-event probes and deterministic installed-SDK parser characterization using a captured real native event. This is not a working SSE fallback or full stream-security proof.
- Chromium/Firefox/WebKit **infrastructure** scenarios using real browser HTTP/CORS, email-link navigation, explicit login and protected writes/reads. These deliberately do not pretend to be the future SDK/example application's complete E01–E15 suite.
- Guard/unit tests and a progress validator that refuse unsupported targets, ownership/checksum/image drift, missing evidence or incomplete signoff metadata.
- CI for Node 22.23.2/24.21.0 harness checks and primary-Node real backends/browsers/cleanup. CI status is not assumed from the workflow file; actual run URLs/results belong in the ledger.

See the ledger's `evidence` and `missing` fields for what actually ran versus what only exists in code. Coverage/release thresholds for the future SDK remain mandatory; the harness is not used to inflate SDK coverage.

## Restart and reproduce

Prerequisites: Node 22.23.2 or 24.21.0, npm, a running **local Unix-socket Docker daemon**, `curl` and `unzip`. macOS/Linux arm64/x64 TrailBase fixtures are supported by the download manifest; only recorded environments are verified. Allow several minutes and disk space for the first database/browser downloads.

```sh
npm ci
npm run progress
npm run check
npm run test:unit
npx playwright install chromium firefox webkit
# Linux: npx playwright install --with-deps chromium firefox webkit
npm run test:phase-a
npm run test:cleanup
```

| Command | Current scope / result expectations |
| --- | --- |
| `npm run check` | Harness TypeScript/script syntax, markdown links/IDs, ledger structure, evidence freshness and secret scan |
| `npm run test:unit` | Harness/ledger/fixture guard checks plus 1000 seeded fixture UUID vectors; not SDK unit coverage |
| `npm run test:phase-a` | Provision real backends, run database/characterization/stream tests and all three browser infrastructure scenarios, then always clean up |
| `npm run test:db` | Isolated real fixture/database tests only (includes 10 pgTAP assertions) |
| `npm run test:integration` | Isolated upstream characterization only; not the adapter integration suite |
| `npm run test:sdk:data` | Dedicated real adapter + official Supabase data slice with owned row/audit/request/permission postconditions; not complete SDK verification |
| `npm run test:sdk:unit`, `npm run build:sdk` | Focused data unit/property checks and emitted declarations; no full coverage/package signoff |
| `npm run test:phase-a:browser` | Isolated three-browser infrastructure subset |
| `npm run test:cleanup` | Deliberately fail setup after both servers start; pass only if owned native/Docker resources are torn down |
| `npm run tools:install` | Download/check/extract the exact native TrailBase archive |
| `npm run test:contract`, `test:security`, `test:e2e`, `test:package`, `test:all` | **Fail intentionally:** full SDK gates do not exist yet. They are not aliases for the smaller Phase A probes |

The runner allocates fresh ports/project IDs/users and ignores hosted Supabase/TrailBase environment variables for provisioning. It uses an owned loopback-binding Docker network and rejects non-loopback published ports; the native HTTP origin also serves a per-run ownership marker. It never resets a linked/live project. One local run at a time is intentional; never bypass its lock to run overlapping resets.

### Interruptions and failures

- Normal failure/SIGINT/SIGTERM goes through owned-resource cleanup. Startup and test waits are bounded.
- After a hard kill, consult the private `.runtime/phase-a.lock/owner.json` and `.runtime/runs/<run-id>/owner.json`. If the recorded runner is still live, stop that runner and let cleanup finish; do not start a second reset.
- For a dead runner, use `npm run fixtures:cleanup -- <run-id>`. Recovery verifies directory/project/PID ownership and refuses unrelated processes, projects or a remote Docker daemon. It checks for remaining owned containers/volumes/networks before clearing a matching stale lock.
- Never use global Docker prune, delete someone else's depot, or blindly remove a lock. If ownership/PID checks fail, record the blocker and inspect instead of overriding safety.
- Private raw logs/reports/mail/tokens are under `.runtime` and are **not** safe to publish. Owned depots/current context credentials are deleted after cleanup; private diagnostics may still contain credentials. Retain only as long as needed, then remove the stopped run's directory.
- Sanitized allowlisted summaries are under `artifacts/phase-a/` and `artifacts/unit/`; CI uploads only these. Reports retain scope, versions/digests, source hash, test names/status and cleanup results, never response bodies/mail links/headers.
- This Mac's Node HTTPS browser downloader timed out; exact official browser revision archives were fetched through `curl` into the normal Playwright cache for local verification. That workaround is not a product dependency or a claim that standard installation worked locally.

## Evidence, approval, and the end-of-session handoff

1. Run every applicable command and preserve its **sanitized** report. The source hash covers Git-visible scripts/tests/config/lockfile/workflow, contract docs, and future SDK/example code; Git-ignored caches/private files are excluded. Changed sources make old evidence stale. CI reports also identify the tested Git commit/run URL.
2. Copy current-source safe reports into `docs/evidence/<descriptive-run-id>.json`; also link the exact public repository CI run when relevant. A URL alone is not machine-checked proof. Do not check in `.runtime` files or raw Playwright traces.
3. Update the corresponding ledger row: status, executed test families/evidence, exact `missing` checks, findings/blockers, and the next ordered steps. Historical evidence remains identified as historical; it must not certify changed code.
4. The maintainer reviews required tests and contract decisions, then records `status: "signed-off"`, `reviewer: "burggraf"`, `reviewedAt`, `reviewedSourceSha256` from the reviewed current report, and no missing criteria. Gate approval must also update user-facing limitations. An automated agent must not invent this approval.
5. Run `npm run check` and `npm run progress`, commit the code/tests/ledger together, and report actual checks, open gaps and the next action. No "done" badge or supported API claim without the feature's own full matrix evidence.

Feature signoff also requires all seven research decisions to have maintainer approval. A fresh replacement report cannot carry an old review onto changed sources: the approval's source hash must still match, otherwise the maintainer must review again. The validator checks structure, references and freshness. It does **not** authenticate a reviewer or decide whether a scope exception is acceptable; attributable maintainer review is still required. CI branch protection must be configured/verified separately rather than assumed.

## Next development boundary

Finish missing Phase A characterization (especially stream authorization/expiry/loss and native auth hydration/refresh/logout races), review G1–G7 with the maintainer, and obtain Phase A signoff. Then begin Phase B's smallest tested client/types/mapping/results slice. No Level 2/3 implementation is authorized.
